[1] https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smith_normal_form#Algorithm\n    .. [2] http://sierra.nmsu.edu/morandi/notes/SmithNormalForm.pdf\n\n    '''\n    dM = _to_domain(m, domain)\n    factors = _invf(dM)\n    factors = tuple(dM.domain.to_sympy(f) for f in factors)\n    # XXX: deprecated.\n    if hasattr(m, \"ring\"):\n        if m.ring.is_PolynomialRing:\n            K = m.ring\n            to_poly = lambda f: Poly(f, K.symbols, domain=K.domain)\n            factors = tuple(to_poly(f) for f in factors)\n    return factors\n\n\ndef hermite_normal_form(A, *, D=None, check_rank=False):\n    r\"\"\"\n    Compute the Hermite Normal Form of a Matrix *A* of integers.\n\n    Examples\n    ========\n\n    >>> from sympy import Matrix\n    >>> from sympy.matrices.normalforms import hermite_normal_form\n    >>> m = Matrix([[12, 6, 4], [3, 9, 6], [2, 16, 14]])\n    >>> print(hermite_normal_form(m))\n    Matrix([[10, 0, 2], [0, 15, 3], [0, 0, 2]])\n\n    Parameters\n    ==========\n\n    A : $m \\times n$ ``Matrix`` of integers.\n\n    D : int, optional\n        Let $W$ be the HNF of *A*. If known in advance, a positive integer *D*\n        being any multiple of $\\det(W)$ may be provided. In this case, if *A*\n        also has rank $m$, then we may use an alternative algorithm that works\n        mod *D* in order to prevent coefficient explosion.\n\n    check_rank : boolean, optional (default=False)\n        The basic assumption is that, if you pass a value for *D*, then\n        you already believe that *A* has rank $m$, so we do not waste time\n        checking it for you. If you do want this to be checked (and the\n        ordinary, non-modulo *D* algorithm to be used if the check fails), then\n        set *check_rank* to ``True``.\n\n    Returns\n    =======\n\n    ``Matrix``\n        The HNF of matrix *A*.\n\n    Raises\n    ======\n\n    DMDomainError\n        If the domain of the matrix is not :ref:`ZZ`.\n\n    DMShapeError\n        If the mod *D* algorithm is used but the matrix has more rows than\n        columns.\n\n    References\n    ==========\n\n    .. [1] Cohen, H. *A Course in Computational Algebraic Number Theory.*\n       (See Algorithms 2.4.5 and 2.4.8.)\n\n    \"\"\"\n    # Accept any of Python int, SymPy Integer, and ZZ itself:\n    if D is not None and not ZZ.of_type(D):\n        D = ZZ(int(D))\n    return _hnf(A._rep, D=D, check_rank=check_rank).to_Matrix()\n"}]},"isError":false}

If you do want this to be checked (and the\n        ordinary, non-modulo *D* algorithm to be used if the check fails), then\n        set *check_rank* to ``True``.\n\n    Returns\n    =======\n\n    ``Matrix``\n        The HNF of matrix *A*.\n\n    Raises\n    ======\n\n    DMDomainError\n        If the domain of the matrix is not :ref:`ZZ`.\n\n    DMShapeError\n        If the mod *D* algorithm is used but the matrix has more rows than\n        columns.\n\n    References\n    ==========\n\n    .. In the algorithms for computing HNF,\n    it is critical that x, y not only satisfy the condition of being small\n    in magnitude -- namely that |x| <= |b|/g, |y| <- |a|/g -- but also that\n    y == 0 when a | b.\n\n    \"\"\"\n    x, y, g = ZZ.gcdex(a, b)\n    if a != 0 and b % a == 0:\n        y = 0\n        x = -1 if a < 0 else 1\n    return x, y, g\n\n\ndef _hermite_normal_form(A):\n    r\"\"\"\n    Compute the Hermite Normal Form of DomainMatrix *A* over :ref:`ZZ`.\n\n    Parameters\n    ==========\n\n    A : :py:class:`~.DomainMatrix` over domain :ref:`ZZ`.\n\n    Returns\n    =======\n\n    :py:class:`~.DomainMatrix`\n        The HNF of matrix *A*.\n\n    Raises\n    ======\n\n    DMDomainError\n        If the domain of the matrix is not :ref:`ZZ`.\n\n    References\n    ==========\n\n    .. The total number of rows we will consider is min(m, n), where\n    # A is an m x n matrix.\n    m, n = A.shape\n    rows = min(m, n)\n    A = A.to_dense().rep.copy()\n    # Our goal is to put pivot entries in the rightmost columns.\n    # Invariant: Before processing each row, k should be the index of the\n    # leftmost column in which we have so far put a pivot.\n    k = n\n    for i in range(m - 1, m - 1 - rows, -1):\n        k -= 1\n        # k now points to the column in which we want to put a pivot.\n        # We want zeros in all entries to the left of the pivot column.\n        for j in range(k - 1, -1, -1):\n            if A[i][j] != 0:\n                # Replace cols j, k by lin combs of these cols such that, in row i,\n                # col j has 0, while col k has the gcd of their row i entries. 1, -1, -1):\n        k -= 1\n        for j in range(k - 1, -1, -1):\n            if A[i][j] != 0:\n                u, v, d = _gcdex(A[i][k], A[i][j])\n                r, s = A[i][k] // d, A[i][j] // d\n                add_columns_mod_R(A, R, k, j, u, v, -s, r)\n        b = A[i][k]\n        if b == 0:\n            A[i][k] = b = R\n        u, v, d = _gcdex(b, R)\n        for ii in range(m):\n            W[ii][i] = u*A[ii][k] % R\n        if W[i][i] == 0:\n            W[i][i] = R\n        for j in range(i + 1, m):\n            q = W[i][j] // W[i][i]\n            add_columns(W, j, i, 1, -q, 0, 1)\n        R //= d\n    return DomainMatrix(W, (m, m), ZZ).to_dense()\n\n\ndef hermite_normal_form(A, *, D=None, check_rank=False):\n    r\"\"\"\n    Compute the Hermite Normal Form of :py:class:`~.DomainMatrix` *A* over\n    :ref:`ZZ`.\n\n    Examples\n    ========\n\n    >>> from sympy import ZZ\n    >>> from sympy.polys.matrices import DomainMatrix\n    >>> from sympy.polys.matrices.normalforms import hermite_normal_form\n    >>> m = DomainMatrix([[ZZ(12), ZZ(6), ZZ(4)],\n    ... Let me just think about the correct algorithm myself.\n\nThe correct algorithm (Cohen 2.4.5) processes rows from bottom to top. In Cohen's algorithm 2.4.5 (HNF via column operations), for an m×n matrix:\n\n```\nk = n\nfor i = m, m-1, ..., 1:   # rows from bottom\n    if k > 0:\n        for j = 1, ..., k-1:  # columns left of k\n            ... reduce A[i][j] using column j and column k ...\n        if A[i][k] == 0:\n            continue  # (or handle)\n        if A[i][k] < 0: negate column k\n        for j = k+1, ..., n:  # columns right of k\n            q = A[i][j] // A[i][k]\n            col j -= q * col k\n        k -= 1\n```\n\nHmm, and crucially, Cohen processes ALL rows i = m down to 1, but only does column operations while k > 0. Let me look at the actual Cohen Algorithm 2.4.5:\n\n```\nAlgorithm 2.4.5 (Hermite normal form)\nGiven an m × n matrix A with integer coefficients, this algorithm computes the HNF H of A.\n1. [Initialize] Set H ← A, k ← n, i ← m.\n2. [Finished?] If i = 0, output H and terminate the algorithm.\n3. [Reduce row] For j = 1 to k − 1, do the following: if H[i][j] ≠ 0, apply the transformation\n   (H[i][k], H[i][j]) ← (gcd, 0) ... via (u, v, d) = extended gcd(H[i][k], H[i][j]) and column operations.\n4. [Pivot?] If H[i][k] = 0, set i ← i − 1 and go to step 2. Otherwise:\n5. [Make pivot positive] If H[i][k] < 0, multiply column k by −1.\n6. [Reduce entries to the right] For j = k+1 to n, set q = H[i][j] // H[i][k] and subtract q·column k from column j.\n7. [Decrement] Set k ← k − 1, i ← i − 1, go to step 2.\n```\n\nHmm wait, but in Cohen's version, the loop is over i from m down to 1 with k from n down. [Initialize] Set H ← A, k ← n, i ← m.\n2. [Finished?] If i = 0, output H and terminate the algorithm.\n3. [Reduce row] For j = 1 to k − 1, do the following: if H[i][j] ≠ 0, apply the transformation\n   (H[i][k], H[i][j]) ← (gcd, 0) ... via (u, v, d) = extended gcd(H[i][k], H[i][j]) and column operations.\n4. [Pivot?] If H[i][k] = 0, set i ← i − 1 and go to step 2. Otherwise:\n5. [Make pivot positive] If H[i][k] < 0, multiply column k by −1.\n6. [Reduce entries to the right] For j = k+1 to n, set q = H[i][j] // H[i][k] and subtract q·column k from column j.\n7. [Decrement] Set k ← k − 1, i ← i − 1, go to step 2.\n```\n\nHmm wait, but in Cohen's version, the loop is over i from m down to 1 with k from n down. {"type":"tool_execution_update","toolCallId":"call_00_ET_UZkXuN05fjq15uyahMCG4568","toolName":"bash","args":{"command":"cd /tmp/sympy-upstream && grep -n \"_hermite_normal_form\" sympy/polys/matrices/normalforms.py | head -3 && sed -n '/^def _hermite_normal_form/,/^def _hermite_normal_form_modulo_D/p' sympy/polys/matrices/normalforms.py | head -90"},"partialResult":{"content":[{"type":"text","text":"312:def _hermite_normal_form(A):\n389:def _hermite_normal_form_modulo_D(A, D):\n539:        return _hermite_normal_form_modulo_D(A, D)\ndef _hermite_normal_form(A):\n    r\"\"\"\n    Compute the Hermite Normal Form of DomainMatrix *A* over :ref:`ZZ`.\n\n    Parameters\n    ==========\n\n    A : :py:class:`~.DomainMatrix` over domain :ref:`ZZ`.\n\n    Returns\n    =======\n\n    :py:class:`~.DomainMatrix`\n        The HNF of matrix *A*.\n\n    Raises\n    ======\n\n    DMDomainError\n        If the domain of the matrix is not :ref:`ZZ`.\n\n    References\n    ==========\n\n    .. [1] Cohen, H. *A Course in Computational Algebraic Number Theory.*\n       (See Algorithm 2.4.5.)\n\n    \"\"\"\n    if not A.domain.is_ZZ:\n        raise DMDomainError('Matrix must be over domain ZZ.')\n    # We work one row at a time, starting from the bottom row, and working our\n    # way up.\n    m, n = A.shape\n    A = A.to_ddm().copy()\n    # Our goal is to put pivot entries in the rightmost columns.\n    # Invariant: Before processing each row, k should be the index of the\n    # leftmost column in which we have so far put a pivot.\n    k = n\n    for i in range(m - 1, -1, -1):\n        if k == 0:\n            # This case can arise when n < m and we've already found n pivots.\n            # We don't need to consider any more rows, because this is already\n            # the maximum possible number of pivots.\n            break\n        k -= 1\n        # k now points to the column in which we want to put a pivot.\n        # We want zeros in all entries to the left of the pivot column.\n        for j in range(k - 1, -1, -1):\n            if A[i][j] != 0:\n                # Replace cols j, k by lin combs of these cols such that, in row i,\n                # col j has 0, while col k has the gcd of their row i entries. Note\n                # that this ensures a nonzero entry in col k.\n                u, v, d = _gcdex(A[i][k], A[i][j])\n                r, s = A[i][k] // d, A[i][j] // d\n                add_columns(A, k, j, u, v, -s, r)\n        b = A[i][k]\n        # Do not want the pivot entry to be negative.\n        if b < 0:\n            add_columns(A, k, k, -1, 0, -1, 0)\n            b = -b\n        # The pivot entry will be 0 iff the row was 0 from the pivot col all the\n        # way to the left. In this case, we are still working on the same pivot\n        # col for the next row. In the algorithms for computing HNF,\n    it is critical that x, y not only satisfy the condition of being small\n    in magnitude -- namely that |x| <= |b|/g, |y| <- |a|/g -- but also that\n    y == 0 when a | b.\n\n    \"\"\"\n    x, y, g = ZZ.gcdex(a, b)\n    if a != 0 and b % a == 0:\n        y = 0\n        x = -1 if a < 0 else 1\n    return x, y, g\n\n\ndef _hermite_normal_form(A):\n    r\"\"\"\n    Compute the Hermite Normal Form of DomainMatrix *A* over :ref:`ZZ`.\n\n    Parameters\n    ==========\n\n    A : :py:class:`~.DomainMatrix` over domain :ref:`ZZ`.\n\n    Returns\n    =======\n\n    :py:class:`~.DomainMatrix`\n        The HNF of matrix *A*.\n\n    Raises\n    ======\n\n    DMDomainError\n        If the domain of the matrix is not :ref:`ZZ`.\n\n    References\n    ==========\n\n    .. _hermite_normal_form(A):\n389:def _hermite_normal_form_modulo_D(A, D):\n539:        return _hermite_normal_form_modulo_D(A, D)\ndef _hermite_normal_form(A):\n    r\"\"\"\n    Compute the Hermite Normal Form of DomainMatrix *A* over :ref:`ZZ`.\n\n    Parameters\n    ==========\n\n    A : :py:class:`~.DomainMatrix` over domain :ref:`ZZ`.\n\n    Returns\n    =======\n\n    :py:class:`~.DomainMatrix`\n        The HNF of matrix *A*.\n\n    Raises\n    ======\n\n    DMDomainError\n        If the domain of the matrix is not :ref:`ZZ`.\n\n    References\n    ==========\n\n    .. [1] Cohen, H. *A Course in Computational Algebraic Number Theory.*\n       (See Algorithm 2.4.5.)\n\n    \"\"\"\n    if not A.domain.is_ZZ:\n        raise DMDomainError('Matrix must be over domain ZZ.')\n    # We work one row at a time, starting from the bottom row, and working our\n    # way up.\n    m, n = A.shape\n    A = A.to_ddm().copy()\n    # Our goal is to put pivot entries in the rightmost columns.\n    # Invariant: Before processing each row, k should be the index of the\n    # leftmost column in which we have so far put a pivot.\n    k = n\n    for i in range(m - 1, -1, -1):\n        if k == 0:\n            # This case can arise when n < m and we've already found n pivots.\n            # We don't need to consider any more rows, because this is already\n            # the maximum possible number of pivots.\n            break\n        k -= 1\n        # k now points to the column in which we want to put a pivot.\n        # We want zeros in all entries to the left of the pivot column.\n        for j in range(k - 1, -1, -1):\n            if A[i][j] != 0:\n                # Replace cols j, k by lin combs of these cols such that, in row i,\n                # col j has 0, while col k has the gcd of their row i entries. Note\n                # that this ensures a nonzero entry in col k.\n                u, v, d = _gcdex(A[i][k], A[i][j])\n                r, s = A[i][k] // d, A[i][j] // d\n                add_columns(A, k, j, u, v, -s, r)\n        b = A[i][k]\n        # Do not want the pivot entry to be negative.\n        if b < 0:\n            add_columns(A, k, k, -1, 0, -1, 0)\n            b = -b\n        # The pivot entry will be 0 iff the row was 0 from the pivot col all the\n        # way to the left. In this case, we are still working on the same pivot\n        # col for the next row. The fix is complete.\n\n## Summary\n\n**Root cause:** In `sympy/polys/matrices/normalforms.py`, `_hermite_normal_form` only processed the bottom `min(m, n)` rows of the matrix:\n\n```python\nrows = min(m, n)\nfor i in range(m - 1, m - 1 - rows, -1):\n```\n\nFor tall matrices (`m > n`), pivot columns whose nonzero entry lives in an unprocessed top row were never found.
